# **January 2026: Bangladesh Monthly Violence Report**

**Publication date:** February 3, 2026

**Source:** ViolenceTracker.org

**Reporting period:** January 1 \- January 31, 2026

## **1\. Summary**

The overall security situation in Bangladesh has seen significant instability in January 2026\. ViolenceTracker.org's automated monitoring system has analyzed a total of 29,951 news stories from the country's leading media outlets over the past month. Of these, 470 individual incidents of violence have been identified.

**161 people were killed** and **1,010 injured** in violence across the country in January . Analysis of the data shows that while 'Criminal Violence' tops the list in terms of the number of incidents, 'Political Violence' accounts for the highest number of injuries, which is a major threat to public safety.

## **2\. Key statistics**

| Parameters | Number |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Total News Analysis** | 29,951 |
| **Identified incidents of violence** | 470 |
| **Events obtained from multiple sources** | 228 |
| **Events obtained from a single source** | 242 |
| **Total killed** | 161 people |
| **Total injured** | 1,010 people |
| **The most risky district** | Dhaka (51 incidents) |

## **3\. Geographical analysis**

The situation in Dhaka was the most alarming among the 64 districts in January. However, the death toll in border districts (such as Cox's Bazar and Comilla) was comparatively higher. Below are the statistics for the 10 most violent districts:

| Order | District name | Number of incidents | Killed | Injured |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| 1 | Dhaka | 51 | 16 | 44 |
| 2 | Chittagong | 31 | 11 | 16 |
| 3 | Cox's Bazar | 16 | 10 | 9 |
| 4 | Comilla | 15 | 7 | 37 |
| 5 | Faridpur | 14 | 2 | 112 |
| 6 | Narayanganj | 13 | 5 | 46 |
| 7 | Gazipur | 13 | 4 | 12 |
| 8 | Patuakhali | 13 | 3 | 28 |
| 9 | Jessore | 12 | 7 | 12 |
| 10 | Rajshahi | 11 | 7 | 8 |

## **4\. Violence by type**

Analyzing the nature of violence, it is seen that the level of personal and social crime is the highest. However, the high mortality rate in incidents of 'mob justice' or mass beatings indicates a crisis of trust in law and order.

| Categories | Number of incidents | Killed | Injured | Comments |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Criminal Violence** | 230 | 114 | 336 | The highest number of fatalities is in this category. |
| **Political violence** | 173 | 21 | 609 | The highest number of injuries indicates political instability at the ground level. |
| **Mob Justice (Mass Beating)** | 35 | 14 | 41 | The trend of extrajudicial killings is worrying. |
| **Gender-based violence** | 23 | 14 | 17 | Violence against women and children. |
| **Terrorism and explosive incidents** | 8 | 1 | 2 | Even if it is an isolated incident, it is a security threat. |
| **Communal violence** | 5 | 0 | 10 | It was limited in scope. |

## 

## **5\. Source Coverage**

ViolenceTracker.org relied on the country's four major newspapers for information for this report.

* **Prothom Alo:** 8,136 news items (130 violence events identified).  
* **Today's newspaper:** 7,029 news items (91 violence events identified).  
* **Dhaka Post:** 6,943 news items (113 violence events identified).  
* **Jugantar:** 6,434 news items (123 violent events identified).

## 

## **8\. Conclusion and recommendations**

Data from January 2026 shows that more people are being killed in criminal activities than in political conflicts. However, the large number of people injured due to political reasons and the high level of violence in districts like Faridpur and Dhaka indicate a deterioration in law and order. The situation may deteriorate further in the coming months if mob justice and isolated cocktail explosions are not strictly suppressed.

**ViolenceTracker.org** is calling on policymakers and law enforcement agencies to monitor high-risk districts (especially Dhaka, Chittagong, Faridpur) and for political parties to prevent their activists from resorting to violence.
